## BATCH EIGHT’S PAIR RESULT IS VOID — THIS DESK CHECKED OUT main OVER THE RUNNING SUITE; A MISTAKE, MARKED — 2026-10-01 (chat 1)

**THE PAIR DID NOT PRODUCE A MEASUREMENT OF `batch-eight`. IT MEASURED A TREE THIS DESK ITSELF BROKE MID-RUN.**
After launching it on `batch-eight` in the background, **this desk ran `git checkout main` in the SAME
working directory while the suite was still reading files from disk** — to write a ledger entry, not
realizing the background process shares the one checkout. **That is the exact law this desk has recorded
before and broke here: “Build in a worktree, not the primary checkout” / “the box stays quiet during a
stamping pair.”**

**WHAT IT PRODUCED, and why none of it is evidence:** the default leg ran 108 files and finished (exit 1,
“NOT CLEAN”), but **`item-storage-full.mjs` (and presumably others past the checkout) failed with
`Cannot find module` — because the file was LITERALLY REMOVED FROM DISK under the running process when
this desk switched to `main`**, which does not carry that harness file. **Confirmed just now: the file
exists on `batch-eight`, absent on `main`.** The later wave of `exit=3221225794` (Windows
`STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`) crashes and the run-suite’s own “killed — background time limit” report are
consistent with the same cause: Node/Chromium reading a tree that moved under them. **The real-looking
FAILs (`cd1`, `cd2`, `cd4`, `fx3`, `fx4`, `fx7`, `item112a`, `item83f`, `item84`) are NOT trusted either —
they ran on an already-disturbed checkout and are not re-reported as findings.**

**CLEARED: the grant** (the run is fully dead — 0 harness processes, 0 `ws-runtime-verify` browsers,
verified). **`batch-eight` itself is untouched** (a checkout does not alter a branch’s commits); **the pair
needs a genuine re-run, with this desk doing NOTHING ELSE to the shared tree while it runs.**

## CHUNKED PAIR — DEFAULT LEG DONE (ONE REAL FAIL), PARKED LEG STOPPED: SESSION-WIDE EXHAUSTION CONFIRMED — 2026-10-01 (chat 1)

**CHUNKING RULED BY FABLE: 5 chunks of ≤25 files, `--only`, one rebuild (chunk 00 of the default leg
only), `--no-rebuild` for every chunk after. Grant: `chat1-batch8-chunked-1b3c1c8-20261001`.**

**DEFAULT LEG, chunked, ALL 5 CHUNKS SAME STAMP (`tree=1b3c1c8 bundle=index-Bps2KMS9.js/616462b`), 0
harness processes verified between every chunk:**
- chunk 00 (files 1–25): CLEAN 25/25
- chunk 01 (26–50): CLEAN 25/25
- **chunk 02 (51–75): NOT CLEAN, 24/25 — ONE REAL FAIL, no crash, same stamp: `item207b.mjs`**
  — “CLASSIFY: every INSTALLED known family is classed correctly… confirmed fixed here, not re-
  guessed” FAILS: `[“Verdana”,”serif”,”expected”,”sans-serif”],[“Tahoma”,”serif”,”expected”,”sans-serif”]`.
  **TOOLS’ successor `e2a5a78` (“fix classifyGeneric’s OTHER direction”) appears to have REGRESSED the
  ORIGINAL fix** — Verdana and Tahoma are back to reading as serif, the exact box-turn defect this
  check exists to catch. **Owner: TOOLS. Genuinely a product defect, not environmental** — verified by
  identical stamp and a clean box before and after.
- chunk 03 (76–100): CLEAN 25/25
- chunk 04 (101–108): CLEAN 8/8
**Default leg total: 107/108 real PASS, 1 real FAIL (`item207b`), ZERO crashes across all 108 launches
when run in chunks of 25.**

**PARKED LEG, chunk 00 (files 1–25), FRESH `run-suite` process, STILL CRASHED — at launch 24 of 25
(`fx15`, `fx16`), `exit=3221225794` both.** **THIS IS THE SIGNAL FABLE’S RULE 4 NAMED: a fresh process,
right after the chunked default leg’s 108 launches with no reboot, could not complete even one 25-file
chunk.** The resource is NOT purely per-process (chunking’s working theory) — it is carrying over
across process boundaries; “a chunk that fails” (not necessarily “within its first few launches”
literally, but a fresh process failing at all after the prior leg) reads as session-wide per the rule’s
own intent.

**VERIFIED-DEAD-OWNER SWEEP, logged: 8 `ws-runtime-verify` browsers, owner node process already exited
(0 harness processes anywhere). Swept by exact PID** (one killed, seven already self-exited). **Box
confirmed clean after. Grant cleared.**

**STOPPED, per the rule — not retried blind.**

seems to just activate the strip menu, which it shouldn’t do unless I highlight text that has been modified with
B-I-U, etc.” And on headings: “the heading icon is not highlighted. If I click the ‘H’ icon anyway, it make the
heading text smaller in steps. Better would be a ‘+/-’ option when a heading is created that hovers over the heading
text on the page when it is highlighted. The ‘+’ and ‘-’ options should also be easily clickable from the tool
menu, but if a user hits the ‘H’ icon, all heading modifications should be undone. Also, headings should not
default to ALL CAPS.” **Recorded as rulings; owner FIX, ahead of 211’s card port.**

**3 · NICK’S WORDS ON HEADING LEVELS, verbatim:** “Let’s go with whatever the standard is for WordPress or other
web-based apps that the text might get exported to (headings are really for web-formatting purposes since a user can
just create headings however they choose to style them if the piece is going to print or PDF)”. **RULED (Fable, from
his words): six heading levels (`#` to `######`, mapping 1:1 to h1–h6); H adds Heading 2 (the WordPress block
editor’s default) and removes any level; + and − step between h1 and h6. It supersedes the three-level default**
(“three heading sizes, H starts at the middle one”, vetoable) that the earlier relay had proposed. Owner FIX.

no walk ever writes as this account. **Also: this adds a 2nd tool on the Postgres public proxy — added to the after-trip

**BUILT, pushed as a branch only (NOT on `main`, nothing run against the live database):** `smoke-account-tools` @ `2b0aca7`.
(1) **`.invalid` is accepted — read from source:** `auth.ts` `/register` and `/login` only trim, lowercase and check non-empty;
there is no email-format validation anywhere on the server. (2) **The shared hash needed ONE small product-source change,
flagged for review:** `auth.ts` had `BCRYPT_COST` and `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` as private constants and an inline
`bcrypt.hash(…, BCRYPT_COST)`; they now live in a new `apps/server/src/passwordHash.ts` (`BCRYPT_COST`,
`MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`, `hashPassword`), `auth.ts` imports them (two constant lines removed, the sign-up hash call now
`hashPassword(password)`; `DUMMY_PASSWORD_HASH` still uses the same cost), and `create-smoke-account.mjs` imports the BUILT
`dist/passwordHash.js` (it refuses with “Build the server first” if absent). `tsc` x1 exit 0; **the existing
`item224-server-hardening.mjs` compiles `auth.ts` alone, so it needed `passwordHash.ts` written beside it — one added line in
its loader; it is 64/64 again (it was 64/64 on `main`).** `set-password-db.mjs`’s “constants mirror” check now reads
`passwordHash.ts`. *This is a server-code change; it ships with the next deploy, under the new ship gate.*

**THE TOOLS:** `apps/server/scripts/create-smoke-account.mjs` — the secret file `~/.wrizo/smoke-account.json` is created EMPTY
(`wx`, never clobbers), stripped of inherited ACLs (`icacls /inheritance:r /grant:r <user>:(R,W,D)`), THEN written; the
password is 24 characters of `crypto.randomBytes`; only a bcrypt hash goes to the server as a TLS query parameter; prints
one word (`created` / `exists` / `rotated`) and the path, never the email/password/hash/URL; a failed insert deletes the
file (no orphan credentials); `--rotate` writes the new secret to a locked `.new` first, changes the database in ONE transaction
(it reuses `set-password.mjs`’s `applyPasswordChange`, so that account’s sessions end), and only then swaps the file.
`apps/server/scripts/smoke-login.mjs` — login (with the site’s Origin) → `/auth/me` → `POST /api/sync {lastSyncAt:null,
push:{}}` → logout → `/auth/me` must be 401; ONE login attempt; prints status codes and PASS/FAIL only.

**PROOF (browserless; a REAL throwaway Postgres via `embedded-postgres`, no dependency added to this repo; the create script
run as a CHILD PROCESS the way the owner runs it): `harness/smoke-account-db.mjs` 22/22** — `smoke-<hex>@wrizo.invalid`
email and a 24-character password; the stored hash verifies and is cost 12, equal to `dist/passwordHash.js`’s; **nothing
printed contains the email, password, hash or database URL; on this Windows box the file carries exactly ONE access entry
(the current user’s)**; a second run changes nothing; a failed connection leaves no credentials file; `--rotate` swaps the
password, ends only that account’s sessions and leaves no `.new`; a rotate for a missing account leaves the old file
intact; `smoke-login` makes exactly one login attempt even when it fails, sends the Origin on every POST, a pull-only body,
carries the cookie, FAILS if logout does not end the session, and prints no secret. **MUTATION SWEEP, 6/6 RED:** no
`lockDown`, the password printed, a login retry, the email printed by create, a failed insert leaving the file behind,
and the sync probe pushing — *the sync mutant first “survived” because its anchor matched the header COMMENT, not the
code; it was re-anchored to the code line and went red.* **A REAL DEFECT THE PROOF FOUND AND FIXED:** `smoke-login`’s
`process.exit()` crashed Node on this Windows box after a live fetch (exit status 3221226505), turning a PASS into a
garbage exit code; it now sets `process.exitCode` and drains.

**NOT PROVEN BY THIS DESK:** the live path (a real `railway run` against production — waits for Fable’s OK); the smoke

## SMOKE-ACCOUNT TOOLS: FABLE’S REVIEW CHANGES BUILT (`smoke-account-tools` @ `61000bf`) — 2026-10-07 (chat 1)

**FABLE’S BYTE REVIEW of `2b0aca7` (relay): good work, two changes, then cleared.** (1) REQUIRED: the last step proved nothing
server-side — logout’s `Set-Cookie` clears `connect.sid`, so the jar’s `/auth/me` is 401 even if the session survived. Save
the pre-logout cookie and replay it to `GET /auth/me`, expecting 401; keep the current step; add a mutation where logout skips
`session.destroy`, which must go red. (2) Refuse `--base` unless `https://` (loopback http allowed for the proof). Noted, no
change: a hard kill between the file write and the insert leaves a file that a rerun reports as “exists” — delete the
file and rerun; one line in the script’s header.

**BUILT:** (1) `smoke-login.mjs` walk is now SIX steps: login, `/auth/me`, pull-only sync, logout, `/auth/me` with the emptied
jar (kept), and `/auth/me` with the saved PRE-LOGOUT cookie replayed (the replay leaves the jar alone). (2) `assertSafeBase`
— https only; plain http only for the EXACT hostnames `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `[::1]` (so `http://127.0.0.1.evil.example`
and `http://localhost.evil.example` are refused); it runs in `main()` before the credentials file is read and again at the
top of `walk()`, so a refused base reads and sends nothing. (3) the header line in `create-smoke-account.mjs`.

**PROOF (browserless, real throwaway Postgres): `harness/smoke-account-db.mjs` 30/30.** The fake server now models the
real session: login creates one, `/auth/me` is 200 only for a live session id, logout clears the cookie on the client
AND destroys the session — and mode `nodestroy` keeps the clearing but SKIPS the destroy. **That server’s walk FAILS — and
only at the replayed-cookie step (the jar step passes, which is exactly why the replay is needed).** The refusals are
proved for `http://example.com`, `http://127.0.0.1.evil.example`, `http://localhost.evil.example`, `ftp://127.0.0.1` and
a non-URL (exit 2, nothing printed); loopback http still passes. **MUTATIONS (run on this version): M7 skip the replay step →
RED (5); M8 `assertSafeBase` allows anything → RED (5); M9 the replay sends the emptied jar instead of the saved cookie →
RED (2).** The six mutants run on `2b0aca7` (no `lockDown`, password printed, login retry, email printed, orphan file,
sync pushes) were not re-run on this version; the proof they bite is unchanged but this desk claims only what it re-ran.

**WAITING ON:** Fable’s OK on `61000bf`. Then: merge to `main`, build the server, create once, `smoke-login` against
today’s live site as the baseline.

**► AN ACL GAP, FOUND BY THE LIVE RUN, NOT BY THE PROOF:** on the real file `icacls` showed THREE entries — `SYSTEM`
(F), `Administrators` (F) and the user (R,W,D) — not one. In `~/.wrizo` the file keeps SYSTEM and Administrators as
entries that `/inheritance:r` does not remove (the same call in a temp directory strips them, which is why the proof’s
one-entry check passed). *Practical exposure was small — SYSTEM and Administrators can read anything on a single-user
box — but it falsified “only this user”, so it is recorded as a miss.* **Mitigated on the live file now (one-off
`icacls /remove:g` by SID, ACL only, content untouched): exactly ONE entry, the user’s; the file is still readable and
valid.** **FIX, for Fable’s review, on a separate branch `smoke-acl-fix` @ `bc171b9`:** `lockDown` now also removes the SYSTEM
and Administrators entries by well-known SID (locale-independent) and then COUNTS the entries, refusing (and, via
`writeSecretFile`, deleting the file) unless exactly one remains; the proof gained a check that gives a file those
EXPLICIT entries first (before=7 → after=1) — 31/31 — and the mutant that drops the SID removal is RED (killed by the
`acl-not-locked` refusal the guard raises; the harness does not catch that throw, so it dies rather than printing a FAIL line).

session end work on production for a real, hashed-by-the-app account — so the incident’s cause is not the Origin check

## B10 INCIDENT: NICK’S PAGE PANEL EMPTY AFTER SIGN-OUT → SIGN-IN — BACKUP TAKEN, ROWS INTACT, LIVE WALK DONE — 2026-10-07 (chat 1)

**THE REPORT (Fable, relays):** after Nick’s live sign-out → sign-in, the Page panel shows “No pages yet” and he lands on a
blank page; not a refresh issue; same account he has always used. **B10’s gate stays OPEN; no rollback without Nick’s word.**
Nick is told not to sign out anywhere until cleared. Orders: (0) protect backups first; (1) a read-only count of his rows;
(2) a live browser walk with the smoke account; (3) PREPARE (do not run) a rollback rebuild from `e66c288`.

**0 — BACKUP TAKEN (a fresh, separately named object; nothing overwritten or deleted):** `wrizo-backup` was REDEPLOYED
(`railway redeploy --service wrizo-backup`; its `RUN_ON_STARTUP=true` runs a backup at start). New deployment
`a09b4f0a-e728-4a9e-a1a8-9835232ff069` SUCCESS; the log shows the dump valid, 667.42 kB, “Backup uploaded to S3…”, “DB backup
complete…”. **Each backup file is named with its own ISO timestamp (`<prefix>-<timestamp>.tar.gz`), so a new one can never
overwrite an older one, and the backup application’s source has NO delete of any S3 object — its only `unlink` is the
temporary local file.** *(What this desk cannot see: a bucket-side lifecycle or retention rule on the R2 bucket — it never
holds the R2 keys and did not touch them. If one exists it is Nick’s to confirm in the Cloudflare dashboard.)*

**1 — READ-ONLY COUNT (a `begin read only` transaction over the Postgres public proxy; counts and timestamps only, no
contents, no emails; the per-user figures are in the chat report, not in this public repo):** **Nick’s rows are PRESENT and NOT
deleted** — hundreds of journal entries and a few dozen projects and sessions-log rows; the only soft-deleted entries are old
(the newest `deleted_at` is in JULY); the newest `updated_at` is Oct 7, 22:27 MT — minutes before the incident’s last write. This is
NOT server-side data loss.

**2 — LIVE WALK (smoke account; a throw-away headless browser at the production URL, the harness’s command allowed this
time; a `fetch` spy recorded only methods, statuses and record COUNTS):** first sign-in landed on `#/page/new` (a blank unborn
page — NOT the last page); `POST /auth/login` 200 then `POST /api/sync` with `lastSyncAt:null` 200 (nothing to pull: the account
was empty). One line written through the app’s own seam: 1 local entry; **no sync request fired in the next 6 seconds.** Sign out
(no “unsaved” sheet): the sign-out itself pushed the one entry (`pushCounts journalEntries:1`, 200) then `POST /auth/logout`
204; local entries wiped to 0, `wz.signedOutHere` = 1. **Second sign-in: landed on `#/page/new` again; `POST /auth/login` 200;
`POST /api/sync` with `lastSyncAt:null` fired, 200; local entries came back: 1 live.** After a reload: `/auth/me` 200,
another `lastSyncAt:null` sync 200, 1 entry. The Journal route redirected to a real page. *(The spy counted only top-level
keys of the sync reply — `serverTime` and `pull` — so per-collection pulled counts were NOT captured; a rerun should log
`pull`’s own arrays.)* **So pull + apply works for a one-record account; what it does for 177 entries is not yet shown.**

**READ OF THE EVIDENCE (unproven): the Page panel’s list is `getJournalEntries()` minus boards — “No pages yet” means the
LOCAL CACHE has no live entries, not that the server does.** Nick’s rows exist on the server, so the pull is either not
returning them, failing while applying them, or the cache is emptied afterwards. The smoke account cannot tell these apart; it
has one record. Both the “landed on a blank page” symptom and the empty panel reproduce in shape.

**3 — ROLLBACK PREPARED, NOT RUN (Batch 9 = `e66c288`):** from the primary checkout only, tree bare: `git checkout --detach
e66c288`; `pnpm --filter @writer-studio/desktop build:web`; verify `railway status` = `writer-studio` / `production` /
`writer-studio-app`; `railway up --service writer-studio-app --ci`; verify `/healthz`, `/auth/me`, the asset MD5s and the
smoke test; `git checkout main`. **Only on Nick’s word** — and a rollback restores Batch 9’s CLIENT, which may not cure a
server-pull fault; it changes no data. No schema change in B9 or B10.

**NEXT (proposed, not started): reproduce Nick’s pull offline and READ-ONLY** — run the REAL server `sync` route against a
read-only connection and feed its reply to the REAL client `sync.ts` in a node harness (as K10 does), and report only how many
entries the client ends with and any exception — no contents.

**THE SIGNED-IN TIME-LAPSE, against production** (the smoke account logged in through the page’s own `fetch` to `/auth/login` — `loginStatus 200`, `/auth/me` 200; credentials never printed or written; signed out afterwards; **nothing was written to the account: the resume entry is `#/page/new`, an unborn page**). Three sequences, each a reload of the signed-in app with the real splash timing (no hold override): **A** the front door (Arbor), **B** a resume
straight onto the writing page (Arbor), **C** the same in Flux. **METHOD:** the old document is marked (`window.__r6mark`) before the reload so a frame taken before the reload takes effect is never mistaken for the new document; sampling runs back-to-back (~80–130 ms apart) for the first 2 s and every 0.5 s after that, until 1 s after the splash leaves; each frame records the DOM facts and a PIXEL test — the share of bright pixels (luminance > 150) outside the drawing’s box, which a page showing through
would raise to tens of percent (the paper alone reads 27–34 %). **RESULT: 71 hold frames across the three sequences (A 23, B 26, C 22) — every one reads 0.00–0.02 % bright pixels, the front door’s doors and the page’s editor are mounted behind but NOT visible; the veil reads `rgb(17, 6, 0)` Arbor and `rgb(4, 20, 26)` Flux, the splash’s own solid block.** The first NEW-document frame lands at 301 ms (A), 341 ms (B) and 348 ms (C) after the reload and
ALREADY shows the splash; **the driver cannot sample earlier, so the span between the reload and the first paint is not observed** — a stated limit, not a clean bill for it. **MERGED TREE: `page-scroll-return.mjs` PASS 9 checks on BOTH legs; `w2.mjs` PASS 21 (default) and its parked variant PASS (parked leg) — CLEAN.** **CONTROL (main): `page-scroll-return.mjs` FAIL 6/9** — R1 and its mutant (the pin exists only on the branch), **R2** (the way-back slot is left naming the Plan board’s capture), **R3** and its second trip, **R4** — **the same six as ledger (29); `w2` PASS 21 on main.** So the branch’s fix still reads red-on-main, green-on-branch on the tree it would actually ship onto. Evidence: `docs/evidence/page-scroll-return-ab4f6428/`.
**SCROLLBAR S4 PROBE.** `sb5` @ `c5186f1d` (`tsc` exit 0; bundle `index-CSSbNkZl.js` 654,748 b); `scrollbar-minimal.mjs` run alone, both legs, grant `chat1-sb5-20261014` (`2026-10-14T20:29:31Z`), cleared `2026-10-14T20:30:37Z`; 0 browsers after. **FAIL 7/44 on BOTH legs, the same seven checks:** S4 “(Fable’s review) stays shown the whole time the pointer rests on the thumb” (`active: false`); the three probe questions; S4 “(precondition) still shown immediately after the pointer leaves” (`active: false`); S5 (prose) @ 1280 px `barTop 245.44 vs targetTop 137.73` and @ 2200 px `251.43 vs 143.73`. (The S5 (script) pair and S5 (prose) heights pass; the probe driver captured **89 frames** over the rest window on each leg, t 4 → 1,470 ms default, 1 → 1,467 ms parked.)
**THE THREE QUESTIONS, answered by the log (the per-frame probe’s own reading):** **(1) hoverDepth** — **DEFAULT: already 0 at the first sample (t = 4 ms): the pointer never reads as being on the thumb at all.** PARKED: 1 at t = 1 ms, then 0 by t = 17 ms. **(2) `active` flips false** at **1,020 ms (default) / 1,033 ms (parked)** into the rest. **(3) the thumb’s own rendered box MOVES: `maxDriftPx` 393.28, first drift at 69 ms (default) / 84 ms (parked).** The first sample is `{top 249.62, left 1395.95, height 81.36}`; the LAST is **`{top 141.74, left 1002.67, height 81.36}`**; the driver’s own read just BEFORE the press gives the bar `{top 138, left 1002.67, height 631}` inside a scroll container `{top 138, left 431.31, right 1006.67}`. So the thumb is on the container’s own right edge (1002.67 = 1006.67 − 4) when the driver reads it, is **393 px to the right and 107.9 px lower** at the probe’s first frame, and is back on the container’s edge by the end.
**READING, NOT A VERDICT — and what the log cannot say.** What is measured: the thumb’s box is **not stable** across the rest window — far from its settled place at the first frame, starts to move at 69–84 ms, and is at its settled place by the last frame (when it arrives is not in the printed row); `hoverDepth` is 0 from the first sample (default) and the overlay hides on its normal 1 s timer. **What is NOT measured: where the pointer is** — the log carries no pointer coordinates, so whether the pointer ends over the thumb’s settled box is unknown (the end box equals the box the driver read before pressing, yet `hoverDepth` still reads 0 on the default leg). Do not read the move as the cause of the hide on this evidence alone. **One coincidence worth a look: the first frame’s offset (107.9 px lower) is the same ≈ 108 px the S5 (prose) check reports as the bar’s top mismatch (245.44 − 137.73 = 107.71 at 1280 px; 251.43 − 143.73 = 107.70 at 2200 px)** — S5 may read the bar before the settle the probe sees begin at 69–84 ms, so S4 (prose) and S5 (prose) may share one cause (a correction pass that runs late). Not tested; TOOLS’ to confirm, and a probe sample that also records the pointer’s position would answer the first question directly. Evidence: `docs/evidence/scrollbar-s4-probe-c5186f1d/`.

**Fable’s order (Oct 16): “fix/splash-tone (TOOLS) fast lane when pushed.” TOOLS pushed `fix/splash-tone` @ `b053fedb` (“measure the veil’s own paint, not the app underneath it”; `Splash.tsx`, `backdropTone.ts`, `splash.mjs`). **THE CAUSE (TOOLS’ comment in `backdropTone.ts`, confirmed by the measurements below): the probe walked `document.elementFromPoint` to find what is “really there”; the splash’s veil (`.wz-splash-veil`) is `pointer-events: none`, so the hit-test saw straight THROUGH it to whatever the app underneath was showing — a journal page’s light `--paper`, say — and chose the light-theme sketch (dark ink), while the eye sees only the veil, a solid 100%-opaque dark block (`--ink-950`) with the sketch on it. The fix reads the veil’s OWN computed background.**
**RED-FIRST (the branch’s `splash.mjs`, a copy, on `main`’s own product — `fp`, `index-CM-bq2go.js`, the build production serves): FAIL 2/66 — “S5b: on a light PAGE, the splash still measures the veil’s own (always dark) paint, not the page behind it — the dark-theme asset is chosen” (`src /brand/wrizo-sketch-for-light-theme.png`, `tone light`, page `paperBg rgb(243, 237, 225)`) and “S5b: the chosen (dark-theme) asset’s own line pixels are genuinely LIGHT” (`inkSample {r 62, g 43, b 18}`, luminance 0.028 against the 0.179 crossover).** **GREEN: `splash.mjs` PASS (66 checks) on `spt`.**
**THE LANE (default leg, one stamp `tree=67234c1d bundle=index-wIDrzKyV.js/692575b`): 24 of 24 OK** — `splash` 66, `arrival-signin`, `arrival-signin-walk`, `th1`, `th2`, `flux-page-ink`, `flux-card-ink`, `bg1`, `bg2`, `drawers-p1`, `drawers-p1-live`, `pw1`, `pw2`, `item170`, and the core set.
**MEASURED, this desk’s `splashframes.mjs` (reload ON a light page route with a 9 s hold; the veil’s own background, the sketch asset shown, the page’s state): `main`, Flux light — `tone light`, `wrizo-sketch-for-light-theme.png` on the veil `rgb(4, 20, 26)`; `main`, Arbor — `tone light`, the light-theme sketch on `rgb(17, 6, 0)`; the fix, Flux light — `tone dark`, `wrizo-sketch-for-dark-theme.png` on `rgb(4, 20, 26)`; the fix, Arbor — `tone dark`, the dark-theme sketch on `rgb(17, 6, 0)`.** (So on `main` the wrong sketch showed in Arbor too whenever the route underneath was a cream page — Arbor’s pages are cream even with the dark page preference.)
**Frames looked at (1280 x 800, committed as JPEG): `before-main-flux-light-splash` — Flux light: the dark-ink sketch on the dark teal veil, near-invisible, “Booting the terminal…” below; `after-fix-flux-light-splash` — the cream-line sketch, clearly legible on the same veil; `after-fix-arbor-splash` — Arbor: the cream-line sketch on the near-black veil, “Gathering your pages…” below.** The other PNG (`before-main-arbor`) stays in the gitignored `…\.claude\evidence-local\splash-tone-b053fedb\` (4 PNGs in all). **WHAT THIS DESK MEASURED ABOUT THE S12 CRASH (its cause is the branch author’s to find on the box): (1) the new splash MOUNTS in clean boots in both themes — a probe polling every 15 ms on a default-hold boot (no hold override) sees `.wz-splash` at 0–1 ms and keeps it for 3.7 s (Arbor) and 3.4 s (Flux), at 1280 x 800 and at the harness’s own 1366 x 768; on `main`’s product the same probe holds it 5.2 s in both themes (so the new default hold is about 1.5–1.8 s shorter — the “animation’s own length” the branch’s comments describe). (3) `sessionStorage` holds only `wrizo-way-back`, which nothing in the splash reads; the branch does not touch `App.tsx`; `Splash.tsx` decides by a module-level `alreadyShown` (“once per app load”) set in the first render.** READING, NOT A VERDICT: something earlier in `splash.mjs` leaves the app in a state where the next full load renders no splash (or the harness’s load is not a fresh module) — a product bug if a real writer can reach it, a harness-sequence bug if not; the author must run the file on the box to see.

**THE LANE (default leg, 28 files: the branch’s own `help-buttons`, `drawers-p1-live`, `drawers-p1`, `flux-page-ink`, `th1`/`th2`, `item211` + `faint-text-sweep`, `cd2`, `flux-card-ink`, `resume-on-open`, `splash`, `font-span`, `pw1`/`pw2`/`item170`, `b3`, `fx7`, `fx10` and the core set): 26 OK, 2 FAIL.** ROWS-SHADOW VERIFY prints PASS under 12 files. **FAIL: `help-buttons.mjs` 7/19** — S1 “the ‘?’ opens THIS theme’s own sentence” for **Outline, Recede depth and Grafts/Links in BOTH themes** (`text: null`, six checks) and **S2 “a second tap of the SAME ‘?’ closes it”** (`s2Open "true"`, `s2Closed "true"`); **`drawers-p1-live.mjs` 2/88** — L9 [arbor] and L9 [flux], the Grafts/Links tab’s own “?” (`expanded "false"`, no overlay, `text: null`). **THE CAUSE OF S2 — measured, with real pointer events, staged (this desk’s `helpprobe2.mjs`; the Templates “?”, Arbor Draft).** First press: the pop is closed after `mousedown` and **open after `mouseup`** (`expanded "true"`, “Ready-made page layouts.”). **Second press on the SAME “?”: after `mousedown` it is CLOSED (`expanded "false"`, no pop) and after `mouseup` it is OPEN AGAIN.** The press closes the pop (the outside-press handler counts the “?” as outside) and the button’s own click then toggles it open: **a writer cannot close a pop by tapping its “?” again — a PRODUCT defect, not a driver one** (S2 is right). READING, NOT MEASURED, for the other six S1 reds: the S1 loop ends each location with a second tap to close it, which re-opens it (above), so the next “?” is pressed with the previous pop still open; in the probe, pressing the Outline “?” (rect `[293,778,15,15]`, 30 px under the Templates “?”) with the Templates pop open leaves THAT pop open and the Outline “?” unexpanded with focus not on it — the press did not reach the button. WHAT covers it was not measured (I did not read the pop’s rectangle); my hypothesis is that the open pop sits over it. **drawers-p1-live L9 is a FIRST press on the Grafts/Links tab’s “?” opening nothing — not the second-tap bug, and not diagnosed**; it may share the cause (something left open) or not.

One grant, `GM_FRAMES` set. Evidence: `docs/evidence/glitch-meter-0e9e2e59/`. Not merged.
**THE LANE (24 files; ROWS-SHADOW 27 renders PASS under `glitch-meter`): 23 OK, `glitch-meter.mjs` 19 PASS / 2 FAIL of 21;** `th1` 27 and `th2` 42 OK, `typing-latency-scroll`, `font-span`, `drawers-p1-live` and the core set OK. **THE SAFETY FLOOR HOLDS, measured two ways:** never more than 3 flashes in any one second across the whole screen — by the independent observer AND by the budget’s own log (`worstGranted 3`, `worstPulses 3`; 28 granted, 16 refused), the observer saw exactly what the budget granted, and the forced bursts really made it refuse. G1 (one key silences it), G2 (Nick’s ramp at 20/60/180/300/360 s), G3 (progress halves it; crossing the goal resets the lap), G5 (a double dip asks for its second pulse), G7 (Flicker Off = no meter; survives a reload), G8 (the page’s rect is identical at peak and off; the layer takes no input) and G9 (Arbor: no meter) PASS.
**THE TWO REDS, READ — a reading, not a verdict.** (1) **G4 (premise) “both systems flashed — the meter AND Signal Loss each drew pulses (>= 15 pulses)”:** 28 pulses, ALL from the meter (`macro` 8, `tear` 6, `shear` 5, `noise` 4, `sync` 3, `dip` 2); Signal Loss drew NONE in the window. So the claim the limiter exists for — ONE budget shared by the two flashing systems — was not exercised in this run: the floor checks pass because the meter alone stayed under the ceiling. Whether Signal Loss is simply random and sat out, or is suppressed while Progress is on, is NOT measured. (2) **G6 “under reduced motion it shows the faint still overlay, opacity following intensity (1 × 0.42 at peak), EASED IN, not cut”:** the opacity is right (`0.42`) but the computed `transition` is `1e-05s` — **`index.css:1160–1167`, a standing global rule, sets `transition-duration: 0.01ms !important` on `*` under `prefers-reduced-motion`**, so nothing can ease under reduced motion unless it fights the rule. **A design conflict, handed up with both readings: (a) honour the global accessibility rule — the overlay appears without easing under reduced motion — and the check’s “eased in” is the thing to change; (b) the overlay is exempted from the global rule (a more specific `!important` override), which spends a standing safeguard on a decoration.** My lean is (a); it is Fable’s ruling, and the check cannot go green on either without it.
**FRAMES — `GM_FRAMES`, five PNGs written by the harness itself (its header says “six at peak”, the code writes `peak-6` and `peak-12` only — one fewer than the comment, two fewer than six); I LOOKED AT FOUR and committed those as JPEG: `peak-6`, `progress-half`, `reduced-motion`, `flicker-off` (`peak-12` stays local).** Reading, not a measurement: **in `peak-6`, `progress-half` and `reduced-motion` I cannot see a distinct meter effect — the framed Flux page sits with its chrome dissolved, and the visible differences are a pale-orange caret block (peak-6, progress-half) against a saturated-orange one (reduced-motion) and a small red dot at the top-left corner.** One frame is one instant of short pulses, so “I see none” is not “there is none”; the harness’s own G8 (page rect identical) and G4 (28 pulses observed) are the measurements. `flicker-off` shows the “...” panel with FLICKER On | Off set to Off, Progress style “Glitch”, Goal 10 words; the page is the same size and place as in the others (x 450–950). If Fable wants to SEE the meter, a frame has to be taken ON a pulse (the observer’s `__wzPulses` log knows when), not on a timer.

## (102) THE BACKUP PROOF, WRIZO PRODUCTION (Oct 18, Nick at the keyboard): DUMP TAKEN, RESTORED INTO A SCRATCH POSTGRES, 8 TABLES / 372 ROWS, COUNTS AND CONTENT MATCH — RESTORE-MATCH: YES; THE FIRST RUN HIT THE WRONG DATABASE (a different system’s) AND WAS SET ASIDE — 2026-10-18 (chat 1)

**Orders: Fable (Oct 18) — “Nick’s go: YES, unzip the PostgreSQL 18 command-line tools … First check that the dump tool’s version is equal to or newer than production’s Postgres. Then schedule backup-proof.ps1 with Nick at the keyboard; he types the address. Report the three lines and stop on any failure; the connector-b server-first deploy follows only on a match.”** Nick ran `backup-proof.ps1` himself (hidden input; the address was never in a file, a command line or this transcript) and told this desk “Backup taken”.
**THE THREE LINES. (1) DUMP: `pg_dump -Fc` 18.4, 692,641 bytes, all of it between 21:03 and 21:03 by the files’ own timestamps — under a minute (the script’s own `BPDUMP` seconds line was on Nick’s screen, not in a file I read); dump SHA256 `f3293fe4…8bbc`. (2) COUNTS, production before = after = restored, content fingerprint SAME on every table: `drafts` 0, `drawers` 1, `journal_entries` 202, `projects` 32, `session` 7, `sessions_log` 116, `story_plans` 0, `users` 14 — 8 tables, 372 rows. (3) RESTORE-MATCH: YES** — `pg_restore` exit 0 in 0.3 s into a throwaway UTF-8 database on a scratch Postgres 18.4 (deleted after), `matchAfter=true matchBefore=true`. **VERSION GATE: the production server is Postgres 18; the dump tool is 18.4 — equal or newer, passed.** The counts did not drift between the before and after counts (Nick’s production was quiet), so the match is exact, not merely “close”.
**WHAT THE PROOF IS AND IS NOT: it proves the dump is COMPLETE and RESTORABLE as of 21:03 — every row, with content hashes, round-tripped (the rehearsal had already proved the same pipeline on non-ASCII text and shown the check can go red: a corrupted fingerprint turns it to NO). It is a point-in-time backup: writes after 21:03 are not in it, so the rollback it gives a column change is “restore to 21:03”, and the connector-b migration is additive (`page_links`, a nullable jsonb column default `'[]'`, with an old-client guard — proven on a throwaway Postgres at `790fce44`).**
**THE FIRST RUN WENT TO THE WRONG DATABASE — and the version gate and the counts are what showed it.** The script’s first pass (20:57) wrote a dump, `counts-before` and `counts-after` of a **different system’s database — none of it Wrizo’s tables (no `users`, no `journal_entries`)**: an unrelated system’s database; Nick’s address on that first paste pointed at it. It only READ (`pg_dump` and `SELECT count(*)` take no write locks that matter and change nothing); nothing was restored from it, written to it or committed. **Set aside, not used, not committed (the repo is public and that schema is not Wrizo’s to publish): the dump and its two count files remain in `C:\Users\nickh\.claude\backup-proof\out\` — it holds real data of another system, so it is Nick’s to keep or delete, and this desk asks rather than deletes.** The second run (21:03) is Wrizo’s: Postgres 18, the Wrizo tables, and that is the dump above. **Note for the record: this desk’s command that listed the saved counts printed the first run’s TABLE NAMES and COUNTS into this transcript (no address, no secret, no row content); nothing from it is in the repo.** **A check worth adding (INK/this desk, not done): `bp-counts` could refuse to continue unless `users` and `journal_entries` exist — a one-line guard that would have stopped the first run before its dump was written.**
**TOOLS ON THIS MACHINE: the PostgreSQL 18.4 `bin` folder from EDB’s zip (`get.enterprisedb.com/postgresql/postgresql-18.4-1-windows-x64-binaries.zip`, 337,444,127 bytes, SHA256 `7effe34c…7858`; the binaries are UNSIGNED — the provenance is the HTTPS source and this hash), 69 files, extracted to `C:\Users\nickh\tools\pgsql`; no installer, no service, nothing in the repo. The zip is still in `C:\Users\nickh\tools\_download-pg18\` — this desk deletes it on Nick’s word once the connector-b deploy is done.** Evidence: `docs/evidence/backup-proof-oct18/` (Wrizo counts, the restore comparison, the two hashes) and `docs/evidence/backup-proof-prep/` (the helpers, secret-free, updated with UTF-8 and the fingerprints).
**NEXT: the connector-b deploy — the match is YES.** Facts for the deploy (one Railway service builds the web AND the server and starts `node apps/server/dist/index.js`; the migration runs at boot): so connector-b ships as ONE deploy, and that is “server-first” for any open old tab — its cached bundle meets the new server, which the old-client guard covers; a server-only deploy would need the branch split. **That reading is this desk’s; Fable to confirm or say otherwise.** Before it: the merged tree must be re-tested — `origin/main` has moved (`page-templates` shipped) since connector-b’s lane at `b452d7d6`.

**THE GUARD (`bp-counts.mjs`, and the same in `bp-restore.mjs`): before any table is counted, it checks that BOTH `users` AND `journal_entries` exist in `public`; if either is missing it prints exactly `wrong database, stopped` — no table name, no count, no version — writes no counts file, and exits 3, so `backup-proof.ps1`’s existing “the count failed — stopping” branch fires before any dump is taken.** **REHEARSED against throwaway databases, with the REAL helpers (`bp-guard-rehearsal.mjs`; the stand-in tables carry made-up `zz_` names and distinctive counts so a leak would be unmistakable): 7 of 7 PASS — G1 a database with neither table: the output is exactly the one line (stderr empty), exit 3, no counts file, none of the stand-in’s names or counts anywhere in the output; G2 a database with `users` but NOT `journal_entries`: refused the same way (both tables are required); G3 `bp-restore` on a dump of the wrong database: its output is its own restore line and then `wrong database, stopped`, no table, no count, no `RESTORE-MATCH`, and a non-zero exit; G4 THE POSITIVE CONTROL: a database with both tables still counts normally.** **MUTATION: with the guard’s condition replaced by `false` in both helpers (the edit counted, so a silent no-op cannot pass) the SAME rehearsal goes RED — 2 of 7 pass, G1, G2 and the G3 output check fail, and the output then shows the stand-in’s table names and counts, which is exactly what the guard prevents.** The earlier full rehearsal (counts → `pg_dump` → counts → restore on UTF-8 text) still passes (RESTORE-MATCH: YES). **Fable (Oct 18): “FIX’s delete-for-good list (`delete-for-good.mjs` + `b1` both legs, `board-context-menu`, the full pair, the two-device test).”** `origin/delete-for-good` @ `bce12067` (FIX: “a real Delete for good, gated, final, and acknowledged”; plan `docs/plans/delete-for-good.md`). **STEP ZERO FAILED: the branch does NOT merge onto `main`.** `origin/main` (apps = connector-b’s `15dbd991`) + the branch conflicts in `apps/server/src/sync.ts`, ONE hunk, the signature of `upsertJournalEntries`: connector-b exported it (for its migration harness) and `delete-for-good` added a third parameter (`acked: string[] = []`). **The textual fix is one line — keep the `export` and the new parameter — but it is FIX’s product code, so this desk aborted the trial merge and committed nothing; AND THERE IS A SEMANTIC GAP BEHIND IT: the branch has no handling of connector-b’s `page_links` on purge** — its own plan (section 4, “Cross-board links”) said that if connector-b merged first, its link rows naming a purged entry would be cleared by the same purge; connector-b shipped (103), and the branch PREDATES it (its base is `b452d7d6`), so its purge code cannot know `page_links` exists — the rule the plan promised is simply not written yet. Merged as it stands, a page purged would leave links pointing at it in other pages’ `page_links`.
**So I took an EARLY READ on the branch’s OWN base (`dfo` = `bce12067`, which contains `b452d7d6`, before page-templates and connector-b): `tsc` x2 exit 0, server build exit 0, `seed-guard` PASS 36, bundle `index-Dt5atrr0.js` 726,027 b.** (1) **Browserless client check `delete-for-good-check.mjs`: PASS 15** — purge only from Trash, shells, every reader skips a shell, a pulled purge beats a local dirty edit, the gate (a bare console call refuses; the confirmation needs the exact phrase and trusted input), the acknowledgement. (2) **Server two-device proof `delete-for-good-e2e.mjs` on a real throwaway Postgres 18.4 + the compiled server — RUN THREE TIMES: 12/13, 13/13, 13/13.** The first run’s one red was **E5 — the resurrection case, the plan’s central claim:** `{pushedIt: true, acked: false, rejected: false, serverText: "", bLive: false}` — the server row stayed a shell and B held the shell, so nothing resurrected, but the server’s acknowledgement was not seen by the harness; runs two and three read `acked: true`. **An intermittent red on the claim the whole design rests on is a RED (the known-flake list is empty; “it passed twice” is retired as a clearance argument)** — a race somewhere between B’s pull, its push and the acknowledgement; not diagnosed. E1–E4 and E6–E11 and all three mutants (each landed, each turned its case red) passed on every run.
**(3) THE BROWSER LANE (35 files — the 23 that mention Trash plus the sync/persistence core; default and parked, same result): 30 OK, FIVE NOT. `b1.mjs` is OK on BOTH legs.** The five: **(a) `delete-for-good.mjs` — TIMEOUT at 903 s on both legs, no verdict. THE HARNESS DEADLOCKS AT H2 and the product is not at fault: its first H2 statement is `window.wrizoDestructive.request({…}).then(…)`; that expression’s value is the pending promise, and `evalJs` runs with `awaitPromise: true`, so it waits for a confirmation dialog that only the NEXT step could answer. Proof: a throwaway copy (untracked, deleted) whose statement ends `; null` runs `DELETE-FOR-GOOD VERIFY: PASS (16 checks)` in 34 s — H1 a bare call refuses, H2 untrusted fill does not unlock and trusted typing does and a reload locks again, H3, H4 a trashed page’s menu offers “Restore”, a separator, “Delete” and asks “Delete for good?” and the page becomes a shell, H5 “Delete 3 items for good?” and Empty Trash greys, H6 the quiet line “This page was deleted for good on another device.”** **(b) `board-context-menu.mjs` 3/56 — S1 (every `softDeleteEntry` call sits inside `requestDelete`) trips on the new `store/deleteSeams.ts:21` (the seed’s `softAll`, which deliberately bypasses the asking gate and has its own); K16 and K17 (“its menu offers Restore (and no Delete)”) now read `["Restore","---","Delete"]` — the branch’s INTENDED change, which its own plan said to PARK with successors and did not.** **(c) `box-tombstones.mjs` T11 1/31 — a new raw read of a board’s `.boxes` in `persistence.ts` (31 against 30 accounted for): it must be in the file’s baseline with a reason or go through `liveBoxes`.** **(d)/(e) `item224a-sync-integrity` and `sync-generation` — NOVERDICT in 0–1 s: `The requested module ‘./persistence.mjs’ does not provide an export named ‘applyAcknowledgedPurged’` — `sync.ts` now imports it and these harnesses’ scratch `persistence.mjs` does not provide it.**
**AN INCIDENT OF MINE, DISCLOSED: running a debug copy of `delete-for-good.mjs` under `timeout 150` (outside `run-suite`, which reaps) killed the node runner and left ONE browser tree — 9 processes, root `9980` — alive. They carried the harness signature, were 2.6 minutes old, their launching node process was dead, no harness or grant existed, and no other lane was running, so they were mine; I killed that tree by PID and confirmed 0 harness browsers left (one empty temp profile directory remains for the reaper). Lesson kept: a diagnostic run goes through `run-suite`, not a bare `timeout`.**
**WHAT THIS MEANS: the app is behaving as the plan says — the writer’s doors, the gate, the shells, the quiet line — and the lane is NOT shippable. The full pair and the two-device test (the E-series) cannot be certified on a branch that does not merge. ROUTING, FIX, in this order: (1) merge `origin/main` into `delete-for-good`, resolve the one hunk in `sync.ts` (keep `export` and `acked`), and add the connector-b rule from plan section 4 — a purge clears `page_links` entries naming the purged entry (and the purged page’s own links) — with a check and a mutant; (2) PARK (original kept verbatim + SUPERSEDED + successor) `board-context-menu` K16 and K17, and give S1 a successor that names the seed’s `softAll` seam as the one allowed bypass; account T11’s new reader in `box-tombstones`’ baseline with its reason; (3) lift H2’s deadlock (`; null`, or do not return the promise); (4) give `item224a-sync-integrity` and `sync-generation` the new `applyAcknowledgedPurged` export; (5) find E5’s race and show it three times green. Then this desk re-runs the whole set on the exact merge, then the FULL PAIR (it touches save, sync and persistence — the fast-lane rule names those), then — on a SEPARATE DAY, as Fable ordered — its own backup proof with the wrong-database guard in place. Evidence: `docs/evidence/delete-for-good-early-read-bce12067/`.** The autism-economy seed’s local dry run still waits on this shipping.

Two readings, not decided here (FIX’s to measure): (a) timing — the budget stamps a grant when it is ASKED and the observer stamps when the pulse is DRAWN; a draw delayed by tens of milliseconds can push a granted pulse across a window edge, so the observer’s sliding-second sees four where the budget saw three — then the ceiling as the observer (and a person) experiences it can be exceeded by a ms-scale slip, which is a product matter, not only a harness one; (b) a path that draws a pulse without asking the budget at all, which the per-kind counts (all kinds matched grants one-for-one) argue against but do not exclude.** Combined with (114): across roughly 30 runs of `glitch-meter` since FIX’s fixes there have now been TWO distinct intermittent reds — G6 once (a pulse under reduced motion), G4’s floor once (4 in a second). ## (120) rhizome-sprout @ b21a2c63: THE LANE IS 7/8 ON BOTH LEGS — THE ONE RED IS rhizome-latency.mjs’s LIVE HALF, WHICH AS COMMITTED MEASURES NOTHING (IT HOOKS `input`, THE EDITOR NEVER FIRES IT); WITH THE HOOK ON `beforeinput` THE NUMBERS ARE IN, AND ONE OF THREE FADE RUNS WENT OVER BUDGET; NOT MERGED — 2026-10-21 (chat 1)

**Order (Fable, Oct 21): “rhizome @ b21a2c63 (rhizome harnesses + rhizome-latency.mjs live half, report every number, Arbor only, no full pair).”** `rz` = `origin/main` @ `35fd073e` + `rhizome-sprout` → **`e0164c28`** (`--no-ff`, no conflicts; `index.css` auto-merged; the branch is 7 files, +693/−227: `RhizomeField.tsx`, `rhizomeGrowth.ts`, `index.css`, the three rhizome harnesses, a mockup). `tsc` x2 exit 0, `seed-guard` PASS 36, readers guard PASS (5 checks, 23 reads), `typed-literal-check` PASS 29, `rhizome-latency` PART 1 (browserless) PASS 5; web bundle `index-DjWsoGk8.js` 766.45 kB. I did not separately check the “Arbor only” scope. Evidence: `docs/evidence/rhizome-sprout-b21a2c63/`.
**THE LANE (8 files — the four rhizome harnesses plus the engine neighbours `m2`/`m3`/`m4`/`j6`, both legs, one grant `chat1-rz-20261021`, 0 browsers after): 7 of 8 OK on BOTH legs. `rhizome-goal-core` PASS 33, `rhizome-goal` PASS 10, `rhizome-growth` PASS 51 (52 on the parked leg), `m2` 26, `m3` 6, `m4` 35, `j6` 33 (rows-shadow PASS, 9 renders). The one red, on both legs and in a direct run: `rhizome-latency.mjs` FAIL 6/13 — and the numbers it printed are `{"n":0,"median":null,"p95":null,"max":null}` in EVERY phase (rest, hold, fade): it measured no key at all.** (The five PART 1 pins pass; so does the premise that the flash holds with the drop-shadow applied to the full field, and “at rest the field carries no filter”.)
**WHY IT MEASURED NOTHING, DIAGNOSED ON THE BOX (a diagnostic COPY of the harness, run through `run-suite`, never committed into a tree; its diff is in the evidence folder).** The probe stamps a key on `keydown` and reads the frame after the one that painted it from an `input` listener. Event counters over 24 keys: **`keydown` 24, `beforeinput` 24, `input` 0**, `requestAnimationFrame` running (164 frames), page visible and focused, the editor holding all 24 typed letters. `ForwardOnlyEditor` handles insertion at `beforeinput`, so `input` never fires on a key and the probe never records a sample. **A harness defect, not a latency finding.** Check D (“no key was dropped”) is a second, separate defect: it sleeps 900 ms, calls `wrizoFlushNow()` and reads the stored text — and `flushNow` does not save the editor (the standing lesson: wait on the STORED text); polling the stored text, it settled and the check PASSED (the poll took 2,292 ms).
**THE NUMBERS, with the one hook changed to `beforeinput` (the diagnostic copy; a key’s latency = keydown to the frame after the one that painted it; budgets in the file: median within 10 ms and p95 within 25 ms of the rest numbers, no key over 100 ms). Three runs, box quiet, every number the harness printed:** run 1 — rest n=24 median 26.4 / p95 31.7 / max 31.8; flash n=14 25.8 / 32.6 / 32.6; fade n=12 26.0 / 53.3 / 53.3 (all within budget). Run 2 — rest n=24 24.5 / 31.4 / 31.9; flash n=10 25.2 / 29.5 / 29.5; fade n=12 26.4 / 37.6 / 37.6 (within budget). Run 3 (the run with D polled) — rest n=24 23.9 / 29.3 / 30.8; flash n=13 24.2 / 31.8 / 31.8; **fade n=12 26.7 / 60.9 / 60.9 — OVER the p95 budget (rest p95 29.3 + 25 = 54.3), under the 100 ms cap.** **READING, not a verdict:** typing inside the goal flash (the full-field drop-shadow, the thing the harness exists to watch) costs nothing measurable — the medians are within about 1 ms of rest in all three runs. The fade is the open question: its median is also at rest, but one key in twelve landed on a slow frame (53, 38 and 61 ms against a ~26 ms median — two to four frames at 60 Hz), and with n=12 the “p95” is that single worst key, so the budget is really a worst-key budget; it was met twice and missed once. Whether that slow frame is the fade’s start/end, a layout or paint spike, or the box is not known from these runs. By the standing law an intermittent miss is a red; I am not calling it a product defect or a harness budget.
**ROUTING. INK: (1) hook `beforeinput` (or the frame after the DOM mutation) instead of `input`; (2) D waits for the STORED text to settle before it reads; (3) the fade: log WHICH key was slow and when it fell relative to the fade’s start/end (and run the fade with more keys than 12 so the percentile means something), then say whether the 53–61 ms frame is the fade’s or the box’s — if it is the fade’s, the field’s opacity/filter animation is the suspect. Then this desk re-runs `rhizome-latency` on the exact merge, several times.** Not merged (the lane’s other seven are green on both legs; the one red is the branch’s own new file), not deployed. Fable’s “report every number” is the list above; no full pair was run.

## (124) sprout-invite-scope @ a7a67d90: THE LANE IS 13/16 ON BOTH LEGS — THREE REDS, ALL ON THE STORED PREF (ITS OWN E4 ×3, AND fx15 S2 / fx16 S2, GREEN ON A MAIN CONTROL); NOT MERGED — 2026-10-21 (chat 1)

**Order (Oct 21): “sprout-invite-scope @ a7a67d90 (its DOM harness sprout-invite-scope.mjs).”** `si` = `origin/main` @ `e489c3b0` (with limit3 merged) + the branch → **`dde065a3`** (no conflicts; 4 files, +447/−22: `useFirstLineInvite.tsx` (+87), `PageEditor.tsx` (12), and two new harnesses `sprout-invite-scope.mjs` and `sprout-invite-scope-logic.mjs`; two commits — the harness first, red on main, then “scope the invite pref per account, stop the redundant door”). `tsc` x2 exit 0, `seed-guard` PASS 36, readers guard PASS (5 checks, 23 reads), `typed-literal-check` PASS 29, `glitch-meter-check` PASS 15, `sprout-invite-scope-logic` PASS 14 (browserless); bundle `index-Cs_ZIDHS.js` 769,248 b. Evidence: `docs/evidence/sprout-invite-scope-a7a67d90/`.
**THE LANE (16 files — its own harness, the first-line-invite neighbours `fx15`/`fx16`/`fx3`/`e1`/`item133`/`item109`/`sc1`/`sc2`/`writing-r3`/`item83f`/`item210`, `page-templates`, `bg1`, `fx1`, `typing-latency-scroll`; both legs, one grant `chat1-si-20261021`, stamp `tree=dde065a3 bundle=index-Cs_ZIDHS.js/769248b`, 0 browsers after): 13 of 16 OK on BOTH legs; the same three red on both.** (1) **`sprout-invite-scope.mjs` FAIL 3/9 — all in E4:** “starts with Sprout present, pref ‘off’” (`sproutDoor: true, invitePrompt: false, pref: null`), “clicking Sprout shows the live prompt immediately and persists ‘on’” (`sproutDoor: false, invitePrompt: true, pref: null`), “a second fresh blank page after the click shows NO Sprout door and the live prompt instead” (`invitePrompt: true, pref: null`). (2) **`fx15.mjs` FAIL 2/13 — S2:** “‘don’t offer again’ withdraws the invitation at once — the prompt disappears and the pref becomes ‘never’” (`prompt: false, pref: null`) and “the withdrawal PERSISTS — after a reload … the pref is still ‘never’” (`pref: null`). (3) **`fx16.mjs` FAIL 2/6 — S2:** “a deliberate opt-in … SURVIVES the migration; the prompt still shows and the pref stays ‘on’” (`prompt: true, invite: true, pref: null, marker: "1"`) and “an explicit withdrawal (‘never’) survives the migration untouched” (`invite: false, pref: null`).
**THE CONTROL: `elc2` (the `main` product before limit3; the branch touches neither the flash budget nor the caret), `fx15` PASS 13 and `fx16` PASS 6 on BOTH legs.** So the two are green on `main` and red on the branch: the branch causes them. (`sprout-invite-scope.mjs` is new.)
**READING, not a verdict, not diagnosed: in every one of the seven failing checks the BEHAVIOUR is right — the prompt shows when it should (`invitePrompt: true` / `prompt: true`) and is withdrawn when it should (`prompt: false`) — and the only wrong value is the stored pref, which reads `null` where the check expects ‘on’/‘never’. The branch scopes the pref per account (`prefKeyFor(userId)` → `wrizo-first-line-invite:<userId>`; signed out keeps the bare `wrizo-first-line-invite`), and the harnesses read/seed the BARE key. So the likeliest cause is that in the harness session the app has a current user and writes the scoped key, while the checks look at the bare one — fx15/fx16 then encode the old global key and need PARKING with successors that read the scoped key, and the branch’s own E4 reads the wrong key (E1–E3 pass because they seed the bare key and the migration moves it). Whether that user is the anonymous account of the F-arc gate, or something else, I did not check.** Equally possible, and not excluded: the write really is landing nowhere; the behaviour checks cannot tell. A pref that does not persist would be a product defect (the invitation would return on every reload).
**ROUTING. The branch’s author: (1) say which key the product writes in the harness session and make E4 read it; (2) park `fx15` S2 and `fx16` S2 (original verbatim, SUPERSEDED, successor reading the scoped key) — and the park sweep must walk every harness that READS the invite pref, not only the files the branch edits (the same lesson as bg1/bg2/th1 and typewriter-scope); (3) show a reload test that the pref persists (that is the product claim at risk). Then this desk re-runs the red files + the lane on the exact merge, both legs.** Not merged, not deployed.

NOT yet: `theme-picker`, `flux-inputs` (deploy #5, on Fable’s clears).**
**THE LAUNCHER GUARD (Fable: “a guard in code, not a habit”). WHAT WAS ALREADY THERE — read from `runtime-verify.mjs` and `box-grant.mjs`, not assumed: the ONLY browser launch in the repo’s harness code is `launchBrowser`, called from `withHarness`, which (1) refuses unless `WS_BOX_TURN` MATCHES the grant file (item 140) and (2) refuses if a live foreign run is in the process table (item 139). So the foreign launches did not come from a code path with no guard; either they presented a COPIED TOKEN — the file is readable by every lane on the box and a match is a string comparison — or they ran from a tree that predates the guard. I cannot tell which from the evidence I have (their processes were gone), so this entry says so rather than claiming a cause.** **WHAT THE BRANCH `box-launch-guard` @ `7369c214` ADDS: (a) the grant is BOUND TO A PROCESS TREE — `box-take.mjs` records the process that CALLED it (the shell/runner that goes on to launch the harness; pid + start time, because Windows reuses pids), and `withHarness` refuses unless ITS OWN ANCESTRY contains that process: a copied token from a different tree no longer opens a browser (reason `not-the-holder`); a holder that has ended refuses as a stale grant (`holder-gone`), and an unreadable process table refuses (`ancestry-unreadable`); a grant WITHOUT a holder (chat 1’s older files) behaves exactly as item 140 did; (b) a LAUNCH LOG — every launch attempt, allowed or refused, appends one line to `~/.wrizo/box-launches.log` (time, pid, ppid, cwd, script, a fingerprint of the token, the verdict and reason), so the next foreign launcher is named by the log and not guessed at.** New browserless harness `box-holder.mjs`: **13 checks with REAL processes — a grant with no holder behaves as 140 (an unrelated process with the token is allowed); with a holder, a child of the holder is allowed and a process OUTSIDE it presenting the SAME, CORRECT token is refused; a dead holder, a reused pid (right pid, wrong start time) and an unreadable table each refuse; `box-take.mjs` records its caller and a stranger with the right token is refused for that grant; the launch-log write precedes the verdict in `withHarness` — PASS 13; `item140.mjs` unchanged, PASS 13.** AGENTS.md carries the rule (“take the box and run the harness in the SAME shell or script”). **LIMITS, stated: it only binds trees that carry the new code — a lane on an old branch keeps the old guard until it rebases; and it is NOT YET MERGED: it still needs a live end-to-end proof on a real harness (a holder-bound run passes, the same token from another shell is refused) — that runs in the next quiet box window, after which I merge it (scripts only, no product).**

## (141) box-launch-guard MERGED @ adf05ae2 — THE LIVE END-TO-END PROOF FAILED ONCE, FOR A REASON THE UNIT CHECKS COULD NOT SEE (MSYS FORKS), WAS FIXED AND PASSED — 2026-10-23 (chat 1)

**THE FIRST LIVE RUN (branch tip `7369c214`) REFUSED MY OWN LEGITIMATE RUN with `reason=holder-gone`.** The unit harness (`box-holder.mjs`, 13 checks) had used real processes but spawned them from node, where a child’s parent is the process that spawned it. In Git Bash (msys) that is not how a script launches a program: every external command runs as `bash(fork) -> program`, and the fork exits the moment the program ends. `box-take.mjs` recorded its parent — the fork — so by the time the same script started the harness, the recorded holder was gone, and the guard (correctly, by its own rule) called the grant stale. The foreign-tree half of that run refused with the same wrong reason, so it proved nothing; I did not count it. Nothing was merged on the strength of 13 green unit checks.
**THE FIX (`fb717541`): record the SESSION, not the fork.** `readProcessTable` now also returns each process’s name; a pure function `chooseHolder(table, pid)` starts at the taking process’s parent and climbs past any SHELL (`bash`/`sh`/…) that was created within 3 s of the taking process and that has a parent in the table; the first ancestor that is not such a shell is the holder (pid + start time). A PowerShell or node caller is returned directly; an old shell (a terminal a person typed into) is the holder and is not climbed past, so two lanes’ terminals are two different holders. `box-take.mjs take … --holder-pid N` binds to an explicit running process instead. The pure function is tested on synthetic tables (H9a–f: a young fork is climbed past; a chain of young shells; a non-shell parent returned directly; an old terminal shell is the holder; a second terminal is a different holder; a missing pid yields no holder, so the guard refuses rather than guesses). `box-holder.mjs` now PASS 19; `item140.mjs` PASS 13.
**THE LIVE END-TO-END PROOF (17:40Z, quiet box: no grant file, 0 harness browsers, 0 harness node processes; one script, `runE2E.sh`, run from the tree of the fixed branch, `fb717541`):** (1) `box-take.mjs take chat1 <token>` from that script — “granted … and bound to the calling process tree”; (2) INSIDE the tree: `run-suite --only fx9.mjs` with the token exported — **fx9 PASS 13, `SUITE RESULT: CLEAN` (1/1)**, and one line appended to `~/.wrizo/box-launches.log` with `ok:true`; (3) FROM ANOTHER TREE with the SAME, CORRECT token: a process created through WMI (its parent is the WMI host, not this shell) ran `run-suite … --only fx9.mjs` — **`SUITE REFUSED: BOX TURN NOT GRANTED. BOX TURN HELD BY ANOTHER PROCESS TREE … Your token matches the file, but a token can be copied … reason=not-the-holder`**, and no browser opened; (4) `release` — “released”, grant file gone. Evidence: `docs/evidence/box-launch-guard-e2e-fb717541/` (the status file, both run outputs, the one launch-log line, the `box-holder` run).
**MERGE: `origin/main` @ `1456da5c` + `box-launch-guard` @ `fb717541` → `adf05ae2` (`--no-ff`, no conflict). The merge touches exactly five files, none of them product: `AGENTS.md`, `box-grant.mjs`, `box-take.mjs`, `runtime-verify.mjs` (the launch log), `harness/box-holder.mjs`. On the merged tree `box-holder` PASS 19 and `item140` PASS 13. No bundle changes (scripts only), so no pair applies — fast lane.**
**WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT DO, stated plainly.** It makes a COPIED token useless from a different session, and it names every launch attempt in `~/.wrizo/box-launches.log`. It does NOT identify the foreign browsers of Oct 23 (still unnamed: INK’s were all guarded, TOOLS and FIX report none, Fable is asking Cursor); the next one will be in the log. It binds only trees that carry this code — a lane on an older branch keeps the old guard until it rebases onto `main`. Two honest edges: the 3-second window is a heuristic for “a fork made for this command”, and a lane that takes the box from one terminal and runs the harness from ANOTHER is refused by design (take and run in the same shell or script, or pass `--holder-pid`). **ACTION: every lane rebases onto `main` @ the merge; chat 1’s own helper `grantsafe.mjs` (take-if-free / release-only-mine) still writes an UNBOUND grant and is updated next.**

## (145) THE LAUNCHER GUARD REFUSED CHAT 1’S OWN PAIR (19:16Z) — the process-tree binding does not survive nested msys shells; found, bounded, not yet fixed — 2026-10-23 (chat 1)

**Ruling recorded (Fable, Oct 23): DEPLOY #5 = `flux-inputs` @ `deeabe91` + `theme-picker` @ `0b66c85d` + `hide-workshop` @ `38e38a0c`; `scrollbar-flush` is DROPPED from it and moves to deploy #6 with FIX’s `page-size` (both global CSS, sharing `--wz-page-pad-x`): one pair for both. The FIX red run (143) is “as designed”; FIX confirms the 14 zoom-premise reds.**
**The deploy-#5 tree.** `b6` = `origin/main` @ `4f733031` + the three merges in order → **`bcfdebbf`** (all clean; `index.css` auto-merged twice, `th2.mjs` once; 68 files, +1,685/−148; `tsc` x2 0, the four browserless guards green; bundle `index-BIASw7Pm.js`, 801.00 kB). The full pair was started at 19:16Z (177 harness files, 10 chunks, the capture tools left out by name).
**WHAT HAPPENED: the guard I merged this morning (141) REFUSED MY OWN RUN.** Chunk 00 of the default leg ended in seconds, twice (the one re-run the rule allows), with `SUITE REFUSED … BOX TURN HELD BY ANOTHER PROCESS TREE … reason=not-the-holder`; the runner stopped by its own rule, nothing ran, no chunk is a verdict, and the grant stayed on disk until I released it (token mine). No red and no green is being claimed from this attempt.
**WHY (measured with a diagnostic that prints the real ancestry, run from `nohup`’d scripts against a throwaway home — never against the real grant):** the grant was bound to the runner script’s own shell, correctly (`holderPid` = the script’s Windows pid, 12556 in the diagnostic). But the harness is started through a function and a second `bash chunk.sh`, and under Git Bash **the Windows parent link breaks across the second nested fork**: the harness’s chain read `node ← bash ← bash(the top; its parent is not in the process table)` — it never reaches the script’s pid. The same chain from a flat script (node started by a fork of the script) is intact (`node ← bash ← script ← (dead nohup stub)`). This is why the live proof of (141) passed — one flat script — and why my `runLane.sh` runs (flat) passed this morning; the proof was real but narrower than the guard’s claim. A second, smaller finding from the same diagnostic: under `nohup … &` the script’s parent is a short-lived stub that exits within about a second; a version of the holder rule that climbed to it was wrong (reverted, not committed).
**CONSEQUENCE, stated plainly: a lane whose runner starts the harness through nested shells (a function calling a script, or a script calling a script) will be refused by the merged guard even with the right token and its own grant — a false refusal, not a missed foreign launch.** TOOLS’ turn (`tools-sbf-20261023`, bound to its terminal shell) started at 19:20Z and is running. **My own runners: `grantsafe.mjs` now takes the grant UNBOUND (item 140’s behaviour) unless `GS_BIND=1`, which is for flat scripts only.** **PROPOSED FIX (not built; needs Fable’s word): stop depending on the process tree. Store only a HASH of the token in the grant file (and keep the launch log). The foreign-launch route the guard was meant to close is “read the token out of the file and present it”; with only the hash in the file there is nothing to read, and the owner needs no particular shell. It does not close the token appearing on a command line for the instant `box-take` runs. Ancestry binding would stay as an optional extra for flat scripts, off by default.**

## (166) BOX DIAGNOSIS: `typewriter-fade` (ticket 164) is NOT the display, the DPI, the scaling, the browser, the date or CPU load; it is a settle race that went from passing to failing at the box reboot — cause not yet pinned — 2026-10-31 (chat 1)

**Order (Fable, Oct 31):** "diagnose the box: what viewport, DPI and Windows scaling the harness gets now vs the passing runs from Oct 30; report the cause and the fix." Measured on the box 09:39Z-09:52Z, on the `ce73d615` tree (clean on this file when stamped), the grant taken and released by the one-command guard each time. Evidence: `docs/evidence/box-diag-typewriter-fade-oct31/`.
**RULED OUT (each measured):**
- **Display / DPI / scaling / resolution.** The harness browser is HEADLESS and emulates its own screen: in both Edge and Chrome it reports `inner 1400x900, dpr 1, screen 800x600, visualViewport 1400x900 scale 1`, identical, and none of it reads the physical displays (the box has two: DISPLAY1 2560x1440 @164 Hz, DISPLAY2 1440x2560 portrait; Windows scale 96 DPI, no per-monitor override). Nothing the monitors do can reach this file.
- **The browser.** Edge 156.0.4314.50 (installed Oct 25-28) and Chrome 156.0.8078.79 (installed Oct 30 04:01Z) were both there for the passing runs; both fail identically now (2-3 of 5 checks, 4 runs).
- **The date / timezone** (the local day rolled over at 06:00Z): CDP timezone overrides to Honolulu, Tokyo and local made no difference (3/5, 3/5, 2/5, 3/5, 3/5).
- **CPU contention / priority.** Idle system load now 0.37 of 16 cores; running the harness at high priority changed nothing (3/5, 4/5, 3/5).
- **Frame rate / smooth-scroll speed.** In-page rAF is 60 fps (188 frames in 3.1 s inside the zoomed typewriter page; 121 in 2.0 s on a bare page); a native smooth scroll of 27 / 136 / 600 px settles in ~100 / 205 / 425 ms.
- **Typing pace.** Slowing the file's per-line pause from 120 to 400 ms still fails (2/5).
**WHAT THE FAILURE IS:** a settle race, not a wrong mask. The file types eight lines, waits a fixed 1200 ms, then +500 ms, and reads. At that read the history is still gliding up (scrollTop ~870) and the writing line sits at y 535-585 inside the bottom of the band, so the mask reads "inverted" (alpha .13). Given time the same page settles at scrollTop 995 and **the file PASSES 5/5 (2 of 2 runs with a ~9 s settle before the read)**. The geometry is otherwise as designed (page zoomed 1.6x, stage 733 px, mask on the page column). So the passing runs of this morning were passing because the glide engine (`useTypewriterFade`, one line per glide, GLIDE_MS 600) used to be done within that 1.7 s; today it is not.
**WHAT CHANGED ON THE BOX between the last pass (05:40Z) and the first fail (06:47Z):** a REBOOT at **06:29:27Z** (last boot), then a fresh login: NZXT CAM, Razer Central, MSI Center, Steam, Battle.net, VS Code started (all near idle now); CPU-Z/hardware-monitor driver installs at 06:04Z and 06:31Z; the AMD chipset drivers installed Oct 30 (the active power plan is now "AMD Ryzen Balanced"); NVIDIA driver 32.0.16.1742 (Sep 29); no font change. **Which of these slows the glide is NOT established**; I could not reproduce a slow glide with any single factor above.
**THE FIX (proposed, not made — it is a harness change the file's owner makes, per the park-never-edit rule):** make `typewriter-fade.mjs` wait on observable state, not a clock: replace the fixed `sleep(1200)` with "scrollTop unchanged for 600 ms, up to 8 s" (the original line kept verbatim, SUPERSEDED, with the successor beneath). That removes the race for any machine speed; it does not restore a faster glide. SEPARATELY and for FIX to judge: if a writer who types a burst and pauses 1.7 s sees the line still gliding, that is the engine being slower than the old pace; worth a look at `GLIDE_MS` / the serial catch-up. A cheap A/B for Nick when convenient: set Windows to the High performance power plan and re-run (I have not changed any OS setting).
**Status:** ticket (164) stays OPEN until the file is green on the box (the proposed wait would turn it green without touching the product). No product or harness file changed by this entry.

(1) Content NOT in main -> tag `archive/<name>` at the tip, then delete; (2) content in main -> delete; (3) KEEP `arbor/mockup-r7` (not a train; merges only via the Arbor ship brief after #13), `cleanup/survey-r3`, `main`. Ledger: every archive tag's sha = the tip before the delete.
**Method:** `git cherry origin/main origin/<branch>` on main `dadb7196`; each tip re-checked against the plan immediately before the first tag (117 of 117 unchanged); tags created at the planned tip, pushed, and verified on the remote one by one BEFORE any delete; then `git push origin --delete` per branch (117 of 117, 0 failed).
